   - Node E is connected to: B, I, P, L, M, J.
3. **Iteratively find other neighboring nodes:**
   - For H:

     - B is connected to: N, C, O, Y, I, P, E.

     - L is connected to: E, M.
     - M is connected to: E, L, J.
     - J is connected to: E, M, D, F.
4. **Evaluate the found paths:**
   - Paths between H and E:
     - H -> B -> E
     - H -> I -> E
     - H -> P -> E
     - H -> I -> G -> F -> J -> E
     - H -> I -> G -> F -> P -> E
     - H -> I -> G -> K -> X -> F -> J -> E

   - However, we need to ensure that all possible paths are considered. By removing H-B, H-I, and H-P, we can disconnect H from E.

After evaluating the paths, we find that the minimum number of edges that must be removed to completely disconnect H and E is 3.

Image name: Tu_task1_gt3_PAIR3
Task: task1_shortest_path_length
Model: mistral-small-3.1-24b
Mode: zero_shot, COT: cot, Attempt: 2
--- Model Response ---
To find the shortest path between the two black nodes (N and G), follow these steps:

1. **Identify the black nodes**: N and G are the black nodes.
2. **Find the neighbors of the black nodes**:
   - N is connected to: C, O, B, Y
   - G is connected to: H, K, F, I
3. **Expand the search to find the shortest path**:
   - From N, we can go to C, O, B, or Y.
   - From G, we can go to H, K, F, or I.

   To find the shortest path, we look at the neighbors of N's neighbors:
   - C is connected to: N, B, H, I
   - O is connected to: N, E, Y, B
   - B is connected to: N, C, E, O, P
   - Y is connected to: N, O, P
   
   Now, check the neighbors of G's neighbors:
   - H is connected to: C, G, I, F
   - K is connected to: G, F, X
   - F is connected to: G, H, K, X, I
   - I is connected to: C, H, G, F, B, P

   Notice that B and I are common neighbors of N and G's neighbors.

4. **Identify the shortest path**:
   - Path from N to B: N -> B (1 edge)
   - Path from B to I: B -> I (1 edge)
   - Path from I to G: I -> G (1 edge)

   Therefore, one path is N -> B -> I -> G, with a total length of 3 edges.

   Also, consider the direct connections from N's and G's direct neighbors:
   - Path from N to C: N -> C (1 edge)
   - Path from C to H: C -> H (1 edge)
   - Path from H to G: H -> G (1 edge)

   Therefore, another path is N -> C -> H -> G, with a total length of 3 edges.

   However, the shortest path is found through the common neighbor I:
   - Path from N to B: N -> B (1 edge)
   - Path from B to I: B -> I (1 edge)
   - Path from I to G: I -> G (1 edge)

   Hence, the shortest path is N -> B -> I -> G.

Length: 3, Path: [N, B, I, G]

### Step-by-Step Analysis:

1. **Identify Direct Connections:**
   - Node E is directly connected to nodes B, G, and F.
   - Node H is directly connected to nodes B, G, and C.

2. **Identify Indirect Connections:**
   - Node E is connected to node F, which is connected to nodes M, X, K, G, and L.
   - Node H is connected to node G, which is connected to nodes X, E, L, and M.
   - Node E is connected to node B, which is connected to nodes N and H.

   - Node H is connected to node C, which is connected to nodes O, Y, P, I, B, and N.

     - E -> B -> H
     - E -> G -> H
     - E -> G -> X -> F -> H (indirect through intermediary nodes, while F and X are additional connections that could complicate the path)
     - E -> F -> (M/L/K) -> (G/X) -> H

   - Removing the edges (E, B), (E, G), and (H, G) will disconnect E and H.

### Cut Set Analysis:
- Removing (E, B) and (H, G) will cut the direct paths.
- Removing (E, G) will cut the indirect path through G.
